Proof of Theorem
3.1
.
Recall that the constants
C_{l}
’s and

M_{l}
’s may vary across lines. By the definition in Table 2, all
pseudo-signals have strong net effects on
Y
in the first stage with

\theta_{l}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}
for a slowly divergent series
\kappa_{n}\rightarrow\infty.
Under this condition, the second part of Proposition

2.1
implies all pseudo-signals can be selected in stage 1 with very
high probability. That is,
\mathcal{H}_{1}
happens with very high
probability. Specifically,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\cap_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,1}^{c}\right)\geq 1-\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,1}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-p^{\ast\ast}\left(n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{1}}-C_{4}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right)

(B.7)

for some arbitrarily large constant
M
, some
M_{1}<M-B_{p^{\ast\ast}},
and
some positive constants
C_{1},\ldots,C_{6},
where the second inequality
holds by the second part of Proposition
2.1
, and the last
inequality holds by Assumption
2
’ by restricting
p^{\ast\ast}\propto n^{B_{p^{\ast\ast}}}.
Since
M
can be arbitrarily large,
M_{1}
can be
arbitrarily large too. The above result, in conjunction with the fact that

\mathcal{H}_{1}\subset\mathcal{H}_{k}
for any any
k>1,
implies that

\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{k}\right)\geq\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{1}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{1}}-C_{4}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

(B.8)

That is, the probability of that all pseudo-signals are selected up to stage

k
is larger than the probability of that all pseudo-signals are selected up
to stage 1.

Recall that
p^{\ast}
is the number of signals that is assumed to be fixed in
Assumption
2
. We consider the probability of
\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}},
the event that all signals and pseudo-signals
are selected up to stage
p^{\ast}.
The key to bound this probability is
Proposition
3.1
. To apply the proposition, we need the condition
that all pre-selected variables are signals or pseudo-signals, which is the
event
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}
. In view of this observation, we conduct the
analysis as follows. First,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

(B.9)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right).

For
\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right),

we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left[1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right]-\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{2}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{8}n^{C_{9}}\right)

(B.10)

for an arbitrarily large constant
M_{2}
and some positive constants

C_{7},C_{8}
and
C_{9}
, where the last inequality follows by equation
(
B.8
) and Lemma
A.13
. Since we only have
p^{\ast}

signals, with Assumption
9
’, Lemma
A.7
implies
that the population effect of at least one signal on
Y
conditional on

\mathcal{D}_{k},

k\leq p^{\ast}-1
, would become large enough to be picked
up by our procedure with very high probability. For the
l
th signal
,

1\leq l\leq p^{\ast}
, we denote the stage where its effect on
Y
becomes large
enough to be picked up by
k_{l}^{\ast},
that is
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k_{l}^{\ast}-1\right)}}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}
. Without loss of
generality, we assume
k_{1}^{\ast}\leq k_{2}^{\ast}\leq k_{3}^{\ast}\ldots\leq k_{p^{\ast}}^{\ast}
. By Lemma
A.7
again,
k_{1}^{\ast}=1
and
k_{l}^{\ast}-k_{l-1}^{\ast}\leq 1.
So the OCMT procedure with very
high probability does not stop until all the signals are selected.

We now bound the probability formally. By definition,

\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}=\cap_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{L}_{l,p^{\ast}}=\cap_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\left(\cup_{h=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,h}\right)\supseteq\cap_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}.

Noting that
k_{1}^{\ast}=1
,
k_{l}^{\ast}-k_{l-1}^{\ast}\leq 1,
and

k_{1}^{\ast}\leq k_{2}^{\ast}\leq k_{3}^{\ast}\ldots\leq k_{p^{\ast}}^{\ast}

if
\cap_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}
occurs, the OCMT
procedure does not stop before stage
k_{p^{\ast}}^{\ast}
. Then
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\cap_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\Pr\left(\cup_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}^{c}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sum_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}^{c}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right).

(B.11)

Conditional on the event
\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1},

all variables in
\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k_{l}^{\ast}-1\right)}
are either
signals or pseudo-signals with
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k_{l}^{\ast}-1\right)}}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}.
Then we can apply the second part of Proposition

3.1
on
\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}^{c}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)
to obtain

\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,k_{l}^{\ast}}^{c}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)\leq n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right).

Substituting this into equation (
B.11
) yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sum_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\left[n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{3}}-C_{10}\exp\left(-C_{11}n^{C_{12}}\right)

(B.12)

for some arbitrarily large constant
M_{3}
and some positive constants

C_{10},C_{11}
and
C_{12}.
Substituting equations (
B.10
) and
(
B.12
) into equation (
B.9
) yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-n^{-M_{3}}-C_{10}\exp\left(-C_{11}n^{C_{12}}\right)\right)\left(1-n^{-M_{2}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{8}n^{C_{9}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{3}}-C_{10}\exp\left(-C_{11}n^{C_{12}}\right)-n^{-M_{2}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{8}n^{C_{9}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{4}}-C_{13}\exp\left(-C_{14}n^{C_{15}}\right)

(B.13)

for some large positive constant
M_{4},
and some positive constants

C_{13},

C_{14},
and
C_{15}
.

With (
B.13
), we are ready to complete the proof of the first part of
the theorem. Notice that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\left[1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\left[1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\cup\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)-\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right].

(B.14)

Note that
\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}|\left\{\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right\}
is the event
that the OCMT procedure does not stop after
p^{\ast}
stages and all signals
and pseudo-signals have been selected. It is equivalent to one or more noise
variables being selected at stage
p^{\ast}+1
conditional on
\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}
, which is

\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,p^{\ast}+1}|\left\{\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right\}
. By the analysis in Lemma
A.13
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,p^{\ast}+1}|\left\{\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,p^{\ast}+1}|\left\{\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-p_{n}n^{-M}-p_{n}C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{5}}-C_{16}\exp\left(-C_{17}n^{C_{18}}\right),

(B.15)

where the last line holds for some arbitrarily large positive number
M_{5}

and some positive constants
C_{16},

C_{17}
and
C_{18}
by the fact that

p_{n}\propto n^{B_{p}}
in Assumption
2
’. Substituting equations
(
B.13
) and (
B.15
) into equation (
B.14
) and applying
Lemma
A.13
on
\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left[1-n^{-M_{5}}-C_{16}\exp(-C_{17}n^{C_{18}})\right]

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ }\times\left[1-n^{-M_{4}}-C_{13}\exp(-C_{14}n^{C_{15}})-n^{-M}-C_{1}\exp(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}})\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{6}}-C_{19}\exp(-C_{20}n^{C_{21}}),

(B.16)

for some arbitrarily large positive number
M_{6}
and some positive constants

C_{19},

C_{20},
and
C_{21}.
Consequently,

\Pr\left(\hat{k}_{s}>p^{\ast}\right)=\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right).

The second part of the theorem is quite straightforward given the analysis so
far. First, we show the result for TPR
n
. We conduct the analysis
conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}
that all selected variables up to
stage
p^{\ast}
are either signals or pseudo-signals. In the first stage, by
Lemma
A.7
, there exists at least one signal with
\theta_{l}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}
. Let

\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}=\left\{l:1\leq l\leq p^{\ast}\text{ and
}\theta_{l}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}\right\}
, the collection of signals that have large effects
on
Y
in the first stage. By the same logic as we have used for the proof to
the first part of the theorem,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(1\right)}=1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\Pr\left(\cup_{l\in\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(1\right)}=0\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M}-C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

for some large positive constant
M
and some positive constants
C_{1},C_{2}

and
C_{3},
where we use the fact that
p^{\ast}
is a fixed number and the
number of elements in
\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}
is
p^{\ast}
at most.
Conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}},
denote the pre-selected variables as

\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}
and the index set of
\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}
as
S_{\left(1\right)}
for stage 2. Note that there may be some hidden signals
accidentally selected in stage 1. We include all those in
\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}

too. As long as we conduct the analysis conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}
that no noise variables are selected, we can proceed the analysis as
usual. Let
\Psi_{\left(2\right)}^{0}=\left\{l:1\leq l\leq p^{\ast},\text{ }l\notin S_{\left(1\right)}\text{\ and }\theta_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}\right\},
the set of signals that have large effects on
Y
with
pre-selected variable
\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}.
By Lemma
A.7
,

\Psi_{\left(2\right)}^{0}
is not empty as long as
S_{\left(1\right)}\neq\left\{1,2,\ldots,p^{\ast}\right\}
that there are some hidden
signals. For the same reason,

\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(2\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(2\right)}=1\right\}\right|\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(1\right)}=1\right\},\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M}-C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right).

So on and so forth. Suppose there are some remaining signals as
k_{s}^{\ast}

in the last stage. Because we only have
p^{\ast}
signals,
k_{s}^{\ast}\leq p^{\ast}
. We similarly use
\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}
denote set of the
signals that have large effects on
Y
with pre-selected variables

\boldsymbol{Z}_{k-1}
and conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}.
Then

\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k_{s}^{\ast}\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k_{s}^{\ast}\right)}=1\right\}\right|\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}-1}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\},\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M}-C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right).

Further,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right|\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k_{s}^{\ast}\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k_{s}^{\ast}\right)}=1\right\}\right|\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}-1}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\},\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k_{s}^{\ast}-1\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k_{s}^{\ast}-1\right)}=1\right\}\right|\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}-2}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\},\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\cdots

\displaystyle\times\Pr\left(\left.\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(1\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(1\right)}=1\right\}\right|\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left[1-n^{-M}-C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right]^{k_{s}^{\ast}+1}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\left(k_{s}^{\ast}+1\right)n^{-M}-\left(k_{s}^{\ast}+1\right)C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{7}}-C_{22}\exp\left(-C_{23}n^{C_{24}}\right)

for some large positive constant
M_{7}
and some positive constants

C_{22},C_{23},
and
C_{24},
where we use the inequalities developed right
before this equation and Lemma
A.13
to obtain the second
inequality. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle E\left(\text{TPR}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=p^{\ast-1}\sum_{l=1}^{p_{n}}E\left\{1\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\text{ and }\left\{E\left[f_{l}^{\ast}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}\neq 0\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=p^{\ast-1}\sum_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\Pr\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right)

\displaystyle\geq p^{\ast-1}\sum_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\Pr\left(\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right\}\cap\left\{\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=p^{\ast-1}\sum_{l=1}^{p^{\ast}}\Pr\left(\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-n^{-M_{7}}-C_{22}\exp\left(-C_{23}n^{C_{24}}\right),

where the fourth line follows from the fact that

\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right\}\cap\left\{\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right\}=\left\{\cap_{k=1}^{k_{s}^{\ast}}\cap_{l\in\Psi_{\left(k\right)}^{0}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l,\left(k\right)}=1\right\}\right\}

for
1\leq l\leq p^{\ast}
. The above equality holds by the way we define

k_{s}^{\ast}
to ensure the effect of each signal on
Y
becomes large
enough at a certain stage.

Next, we turn to FPR
{}_{n}.

\displaystyle E\left(\text{FPR}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(p_{n}-p^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}E\left\{1\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\text{ and }\left\{E\left[f_{l}^{\ast}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}=0\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(p_{n}-p^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}E\left[1\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right)\right]+\left(p_{n}-p^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{n}\Pr\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p^{\ast\ast}}{p_{n}-p^{\ast}}+\left(p_{n}-p^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left[\Pr\left((\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1)\cap\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)+\Pr\left((\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1)\cap\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p^{\ast\ast}}{p_{n}-p^{\ast}}+\left(p_{n}-p^{\ast}\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left[\Pr\left(\left.(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1)\right|\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)+\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p^{\ast\ast}}{p_{n}-p^{\ast}}+\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)+\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{\ast\ast}}{p_{n}-p^{\ast}}+\left[1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}|\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\right]\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)+\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p^{\ast\ast}}{p_{n}-p^{\ast}}+n^{-M_{7}}+C_{22}\exp\left(-C_{23}n^{C_{24}}\right),

for some large positive constant
M_{7}
and positive constants
C_{22},C_{23},
and
C_{24},
where the fifth line holds by the fact that
conditioning on
\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}},

\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{p^{\ast}}
(conditional on the OCMT procedure stops before stage

p^{\ast},
whether one or more noise variables selected by OCMT is a subset
of whether one or more noise variables selected before stage
p^{\ast}
), and
the last inequality holds by applying Lemma
A.13
and equation
(
B.15
).

Now, we turn to FDR
{}_{n}.
By the same analysis for FPR
{}_{n},
we have

\displaystyle E\left[\sum_{l=1}^{p_{n}}1\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1,\text{
}\left\{E\left[f_{l}^{\ast}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}=0,\text{ and }\theta_{l}\lesssim\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}E\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left[\Pr\left((\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1)\cap\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)+\Pr\left((\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1)\cap\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-M_{7}}+C_{22}\exp\left(-C_{23}n^{C_{24}}\right)=o(1).

Then

\text{FDR}_{n}=\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{p_{n}}1\left(\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}=1,\text{ }\left\{E\left[f_{l}^{\ast}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}=0,\text{ and }\theta_{l}\lesssim\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}\right)}{\sum_{l=1}^{p_{n}}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}+1}\overset{P}{\rightarrow}0.

by Markov inequality and the fact that
\sum_{l=1}^{p_{n}}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{l}+1\geq 1
.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.
For (i), we denote the event of the
desirable results from the OCMT procedure as

\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}=\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}},

which is the event that the OCMT concludes at or before stage
p^{\ast},\
all
the signals and pseudo signals are selected, and none of the noise variables
are included. By the proof of Theorem
3.1
(see esp.
equations (
B.13
) and (
B.15
)) and by applying Lemma

A.13
on
\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}^{c}\right),
we
have

\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)=1-o\left(1\right).

(B.17)

Conditional on
\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}},
we claim that all the technical
conditions in Theorem 3 of
Huang, Horowitz and Wei 2010
(HHW) are satisfied. For a
better exposition, we defer the proof of this claim to the end of the proof.

Now, we denote the desirable event of the adaptive group Lasso as

\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}=\left\{\text{All signals are selected, no
pseudo-signals or noise variables are selected}\right\}\text{.}

Theorem 3 of HHW implies that

\Pr\left(\left.\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right|\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)=1-o\left(1\right).

(B.18)

Consequently we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\left.\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right|\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)+\Pr\left(\left.\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right|\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}^{c}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\left.\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right|\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=1-o\left(1\right),

where we use the results in equations (
B.17
) and (
B.18
).

We turn to (ii). By the result in (i),
\Pr\left(\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right)>1-\varepsilon/2
for any fixed small positive value
\varepsilon

after some large
n
. Conditional on
\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}},
the post
OCMT estimation is simply a special case of
Stone 1985
because
p^{\ast}
is
fixed. Further, the bias term is of the order
m_{n}^{-d}
. Since
m_{n}^{-d}\ll\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}
by Assumption
7
. That is, the
bias term is asymptotically negligible. Then the results in
Stone 1985
, we
have conditional on
\mathcal{M}_{\text{AGLASSO}},

P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\hat{\beta}}_{\text{post}}-{\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)=O_{P}\left(\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}\right).

(B.19)

Then unconditionally, we have
P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}_{\text{AGLASSO}}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\hat{\beta}}_{\text{post}}-{\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)=O_{P}\left(\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}\right)
as we can make
\varepsilon
arbitrarily small.
13
13

13

This
holds because for any events
A
and
B\
with
\Pr\left(B\right)>1-\varepsilon/2
and
\Pr\left(A|B\right)>1-\varepsilon/2,
we have

\Pr\left(A\right)=\Pr\left(A|B\right)\Pr\left(B\right)+\Pr\left(A|B^{c}\right)\Pr\left(B^{c}\right)\geq\Pr\left(A|B\right)\Pr\left(B\right)>1-\varepsilon.

.

We complete the proof by demonstrating the initial claim. To achieve this, we
need to verify the conditions for the tuning parameters and conditions A1–A4
as specified in HHW.

To facilitate reading, we present A1–A4 in HHW using our notation.

A1

The number of nonzero components
p^{\ast}
is fixed, and there is a
constant
C_{f}>0
such that
\min_{1\leq j\leq p^{\ast}}\left\{E\left[f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}\geq C_{f}
.

A2

The random variables
\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n}
are
independent and identically distributed with
E\left(\varepsilon_{i}\right)=0
and
\operatorname{Var}\left(\varepsilon_{i}\right)=\sigma^{2}
.
Furthermore, their tail probabilities satisfy
\Pr\left(\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|>t\right)\leq K\exp\left(-Ct^{2}\right),

i=1,\ldots,n
, for all
t\geq 0
and for constants
K
and
C
.

A3

Ef_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)=0,

j=1,\ldots,p^{\ast}
.

A4

The covariate vector
X
has a continuous density and there exist
constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that the density function
g_{j}
of

X_{j}
satisfies
0<C_{1}\leq g_{j}(x)\leq C_{2}<

\infty
on
[0,1]
for
every
1\leq j\leq p_{n}.

We verify those conditions as follows.

•

First, conditional on
\mathcal{M}_{\text{OCMT}}
, all signals and
pseudo signals are selected, while none of the noise variables are included.
Consequently, the regular rank condition, as imposed in Assumption

10
, holds. Furthermore, the number of covariates is
p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}
, which is of the same order as
p^{\ast\ast}
when

p^{\ast\ast}>0
because
p^{\ast}
is fixed.

•

Second, we discuss the tuning parameters. The condition
\lambda_{n1}\geq C\sqrt{n\log(p^{\ast\ast}m_{n})}
was directly imposed in Theorem 1
of HHW. The condition
\lambda_{n1}\ll\sqrt{n/m_{n}}
is stronger than the one
imposed in part (ii) of Theorem 1 of HHW. Thus,
\lambda_{n1}
satisfies the
requirements in HHW. For
\lambda_{n2}
, we only need to verify whether it
satisfies condition (B2) in HHW. The condition
\lambda_{n2}\ll nm_{n}^{-1/4}

clearly satisfies B2(a) in HHW. For B2(b) in HHW, firstly,
r_{n}\propto\sqrt{n/[m_{n}\log(p^{\ast\ast}m_{n})]}
in our case (the convergence rate
ensured by Theorem 1 of HHW), because
\lambda_{n1}\ll\sqrt{n/m_{n}}
and the
bias term
m_{n}^{-d}\ll\sqrt{m_{n}/n}
as ensured by Assumption
7
.
Using the rate of
r_{n}
, some simple calculations show that
\lambda_{n2}\gg m_{n}^{1/2}\log(p^{\ast\ast}m_{n})
satisfies B2(b), again using Assumption

7
. We have verified the conditions required for the tuning parameters.

•

Third, condition A3 in HHW is merely a normalization, and we conduct
such a normalization as well. The major parts of conditions A1, A2, and A4 in
HHW have been imposed in this paper except for two conditions:

1.

HHW impose
\min_{1\leq j\leq p^{\ast}}\left\|f_{j}^{\ast}\right\|\geq C>0
, while our counterpart is
\left\{E[f_{j}^{\ast}(X_{j})^{2}]\right\}^{1/2}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log(m_{n})^{1/2}(m_{n}/n)^{1/2}
for
j=1,2,...,p^{\ast}
as in Assumption 9’.

2.

HHW assume
\Pr(\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|>t)\leq K\exp(-Ct^{2})
for some positive constants
K
and
C
and for all
t
, while
we assume
\Pr(\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|>t)\leq K\exp(-Ct^{s})

for some constant
s>0
in Assumption
4
. Clearly, our conditions
are weaker.

We are going to show that these two conditions have no impact on the final results.

For 1.

As stated in the second bullet point, the rate of convergence of
the Lasso estimator is
r_{n}^{-1}\propto\sqrt{m_{n}\log(p^{\ast\ast}m_{n})/n}
. Note that
\log(p^{\ast\ast}m_{n})\propto\log(m_{n})
under our
framework, because
m_{n}\propto n^{B_{m}}
and
p^{\ast\ast}\propto n^{B_{p^{\ast\ast}}}
. Therefore,
\{E[f_{j}^{\ast}(X_{j})^{2}]\}^{1/2}\gg r_{n}^{-1}
for
j=1,2,...,p^{\ast}
, due to the fact that
\kappa_{n}\rightarrow\infty
. This result implies that the Lasso estimator of
signals will not be zero with very high probability because the signal
strength is much greater than the convergence rate. Therefore, this weaker
condition has no impact on the Lasso shrinkage result.

For 2.

Our tail condition slightly generalizes the one in HHW. The
major use of this condition in HHW is the probability bounds derived in Lemma
2 of HHW. In particular, only the second part of this lemma is useful for us,
viz., the result when
\frac{m_{n}\log(pm_{m})}{n}\rightarrow 0
, the case in
this paper. To guarantee this result for our case, we can apply Lemma A.2 in
this paper by setting
v_{n}=Cn^{1/2}m_{m}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(p_{n}m_{n})}
for
a sufficiently large constant
C
on the series
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x_{ki})\varepsilon_{i}
for
j=1,...,m_{n}
and
k=1,...,p_{n}
. The
probability bound from this lemma is
\exp\left[-\frac{C^{2}(1-\pi)^{2}\log(p_{n}m_{n})}{2C_{1}}\right]
for another uniformly bounded
C_{1}
, for

j=1,...,m_{n}
and
k=1,...,p_{n}
. We set
C
large enough so that the
probability bound is smaller than
(p_{n}m_{n})^{-M}
for some
M>1
. Then

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{j=1,...,m_{n},k=1,...,p_{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x_{ki})\varepsilon_{i}\right|>v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{p_{n}}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x_{ki})\varepsilon_{i}\right|>v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq m_{n}p_{n}(p_{n}m_{n})^{-M}\rightarrow 0,

which implies
\max_{j=1,...,m_{n},k=1,...,p_{n}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x_{ki})\varepsilon_{i}\right|=O_{P}\left[n^{1/2}m_{m}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(p_{n}m_{n})}\right]
, an equivalent result to the second
part of Lemma 2 in HHW.

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.
The uniform boundedness of
\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}\left(X_{j}\right)
implies that
U_{l}
also
satisfies the tail condition in Assumption
4
. [For details, see
the proof of Lemma
A.5
.] This, in conjunction with the
additional conditions in Assumption
2
”, implies that
all technical lemmas can go through. Consequently, we only need to show that
the probability bounds for the events like
\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}},\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}},\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}},
and
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}

are still
1-o\left(1\right)
with a diverging
p^{\ast}.
This statement
holds due to the fact that the error bounds obtained before are of the order
either
n^{-M}
or
\exp\left(-n^{C}\right)
for some large positive
constant
M
and some
C>0,
and the error probabilities accumulated for a
diverging
p^{\ast}
are of the order
p^{\ast}\left(n^{-M}+\exp\left(-n^{C}\right)\right)=o\left(1\right)
. We provide some details below.

We continue to use
M
to denote some large positive constant and
C
to
denote some generic positive constant. For
\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}},
equations
(
B.7
) and (
B.8
) imply that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pr\left(\mathcal{H}_{1}\right)=\Pr\left(\cap_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\mathcal{B}_{l,1}^{c}\right)\geq 1-\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+1}^{p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,1}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-p^{\ast\ast}\left(n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{1}}-C_{4}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

For
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}},

\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{2}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{8}n^{C_{9}}\right)

holds by Lemma
A.13
because
p^{\ast}\lesssim n^{B_{p^{\ast}}}
. Similarly,

\Pr\left(\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}}\cap\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{3}}-C_{10}\exp\left(-C_{11}n^{C_{12}}\right)

for the same reason as we obtain equation (
B.13
) and
p^{\ast}\lesssim n^{B_{p^{\ast}}}
. The probability bound for
\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}
was derived based on the bounds for
\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}},\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}},
and
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}.
Since the probability bounds for

\mathcal{A}_{p^{\ast}},\mathcal{H}_{p^{\ast}},
and
\mathcal{D}_{p^{\ast}}

have been shown, the probability bound for
\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}
can be
obtained using the same idea as we get equation (
B.16
). That is,

\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{4}}-C_{13}\exp\left(-C_{14}n^{C_{15}}\right).

Then the first result is reached by observing that
\Pr\left(\hat{k}_{s}>p^{\ast}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{T}_{p^{\ast}}\right).
The
results of TPR, FPR, and FDR can be proved similarly as in the proof of
Theorem
3.1
and thus omitted.

Appendix S1
Proofs of the Technical Lemmas

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.
To prove the lemma, we first present two
inequalities: for any
m_{n}\times m_{n}
symmetric matrices
\boldsymbol{A}

and
\boldsymbol{B}
, we have

\max\left\{\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right|,\left|\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right|\right\}\leq m_{n}\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty},

(S1.1)

and

\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}\right)\right|\leq\max\left\{\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A-B}\right)\right|,\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B-A}\right)\right|\right\}.

(S1.2)

We will prove them at the end of this proof.

Recall that
\Phi_{X_{l}}=E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)^{\prime}\right].
Let
\Phi_{n,l}=n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}.
The
\left(j,k\right)
-th entry of

\Phi_{n,l}-\Phi_{l}
is
\xi_{jk,l}\equiv n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}\left(x_{li}\right)\phi_{k}\left(x_{li}\right)-E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)\phi_{k}\left(X_{l}\right)\right].
Notice that

\text{var}\left(\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)\phi_{k}\left(X_{l}\right)\right)\leq E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\phi_{k}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\leq E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\leq B_{4}m_{n}^{-1},

where the second inequality holds by
\left\|\phi_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1,
and the last inequality holds by Lemma
A.3
. By Lemma

A.1
,

\Pr\left(\left|\xi_{jk,l}\right|\geq v_{n}/n\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-v_{n}^{2}/\left[2\left(B_{4}nm_{n}^{-1}+v_{n}/3\right)\right]\right\}.

Then by the union bound,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|\Phi_{n,l}-\Phi_{X_{l}}\right\|_{\infty}\geq v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\max_{j,k=1,2,\ldots,m_{n}}\left|\xi_{jk,l}\right|\geq v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left|\xi_{jk,l}\right|\geq v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-v_{n}^{2}/[2\left(B_{4}nm_{n}^{-1}+v_{n}/3\right)]\right\}.

(S1.3)

Note that
\left\|\Phi_{n,l}^{-1}\right\|-\left\|\Phi_{X_{l}}^{-1}\right\|=\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)\right]^{-1}-\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right]^{-1}.
By
(
S1.1
), (
S1.2
), and (
S1.3
),

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right|\geq m_{n}v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\max\left\{\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}-\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right|,\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}-\Phi_{n,l}\right)\right|\right\}\geq m_{n}v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\left\|\Phi_{n,l}-\Phi_{X_{l}}\right\|_{\infty}\geq v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-v_{n}^{2}/\left[2\left(B_{4}nm_{n}^{-1}+v_{n}/3\right)\right]\right\}.

(S1.4)

Set
v_{n}=nm_{n}^{-2}B_{1}/3.
Then the above inequality becomes

\Pr\left(\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right|\geq B_{1}m_{n}^{-1}/3\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{1}nm_{n}^{-3}\right\}

(S1.5)

for some constant
C_{1}
because
nm_{n}^{-1}\gg v_{n}=nm_{n}^{-2}B_{1}/3.

This, in conjunction with the fact that
B_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{l}\right)\leq B_{2}m_{n}^{-1}\
by Lemma
A.3
,
implies

\Pr\left(\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right|\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)/3\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{1}nm_{n}^{-3}\right\}.

(S1.6)

Note that for two positive random variables
a
and
b,

\left|a-b\right|\geq b/2
is equivalent to
\{a-3b/2\geq 0
or
a-b/2

\leq 0\},
which is equivalent to

b^{-1}-a^{-1}\geq b^{-1}/3\text{ \ \ or \ \ }b^{-1}-a^{-1}\leq-b^{-1}.

Therefore,
\left\{\left|a-b\right|\geq b/2\right\}
implies

\left\{\left|b^{-1}-a^{-1}\right|\geq b^{-1}/3\right\},
and

\Pr\left(\left\{\left|a-b\right|\geq b/2\right\}\right)\leq\Pr\left(\left|b^{-1}-a^{-1}\right|\geq b^{-1}/3\right).

Taking
a=\left\{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)\right\}^{-1}

and
b=\left\{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right\}^{-1},

we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)\right]^{-1}-\left[\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right]^{-1}\right|\geq\left\{\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{l}\right)\right\}^{-1}/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left\{\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{n,l}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{X_{l}}\right)\right|\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{l}\right)/3\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{2}nm_{n}^{-3}\right\},

where the last inequality holds by (
S1.6
). This shows the first
part of the lemma.

If Assumption
7
also holds, then

\displaystyle m_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{2}nm_{n}^{-3}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{-C_{2}nm_{n}^{-3}+2\log m_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{-C_{2}n^{1-3B_{m}}+2B_{m}\log n\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-C_{3}n^{C_{4}}\right\}

for some
C_{4}\in(0,1-3B_{m})
and
C_{3}\in(0,C_{2}]
. Then the second part
of the lemma follows.

To complete the proof the lemma, we now show (
S1.2
) and
(
S1.1
). To see equation (
S1.2
), note that for any
vector
\boldsymbol{x=}\left(x_{1},...,x_{m_{n}}\right)^{\prime}
with

\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1,

\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Ax}=\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\left(\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Bx+x}^{\prime}\left(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B}\right)\boldsymbol{x}\right)\geq\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Bx}+\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\left(\boldsymbol{A}-\boldsymbol{B}\right)x},

which is equivalent to

\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}\right)+\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A-B}\right)\ \text{or equivalently }\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A-B}\right)\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}\right).

Switching
\boldsymbol{A}
and
\boldsymbol{B}
yields
\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B-A}\right)\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right).
Therefore

\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A-B}\right)\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}\right)\leq-\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B-A}\right),

and equation (
S1.2
) follows. For (
S1.1
), we have by
Jensen inequality

\left|\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right|=\left|\max_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Ax}\right|\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty}\max_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left|x_{j}\right|\right)^{2}\leq m_{n}\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty}\max_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}x_{j}^{2}=m_{n}\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty},

and similarly

\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right|=\left|\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Ax}\right|\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty}\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left|x_{j}\right|\right)^{2}\leq m_{n}\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty}\min_{\left\|\boldsymbol{x}\right\|=1}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}x_{j}^{2}=m_{n}\left\|\boldsymbol{A}\right\|_{\infty}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.
Noting that
\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}(I_{n}-\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime})\boldsymbol{u}_{l},
we have

\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}-\sigma_{l}^{2}=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}-\sigma_{l}^{2}-n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}-\sigma_{l}^{2}\right|\geq v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}-\sigma_{l}^{2}\right|\geq\left(1-\pi_{1}\right)v_{n}/n\right)

(S1.7)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right|\geq\pi_{1}v_{n}/n\right)

for any
\pi_{1}\in\left(0,1\right).

We first bound the first term on the right hand side of (
S1.7
).
By Assumption
6
and

\displaystyle U_{l}

\displaystyle=Y-P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)-P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{l}+\varepsilon

with

\boldsymbol{\beta}_{l}=\left[E\left(P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)^{\prime}\right)\right]^{-1}E\left(P^{m_{n}}\left(X_{l}\right)Y\right),

we can say that
U_{l}
is just a uniformly bounded random term plus

\varepsilon
because elements in
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{l}
are uniformly
bounded due to Lemma
A.3
and the uniform boundedness of
\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right),
and for each
x
only a
finite elements of
P^{m_{n}}\left(x\right)
are nonzero due to the usage
of finite order B-splines. This implies that
U_{l}
shares the same tail
behavior as
\varepsilon
and satisfies Assumption
4
. Then, the
conditions in Lemma
A.2
hold for
n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}-\sigma_{l}^{2}
with
\alpha=s/2
by
Assumption
4
. Applying Lemma
A.2
yields
that for any
v_{n}\propto n^{\lambda}
with
1/2<\lambda\leq\left(1+s/2\right)/(2+s/2),

\pi<\pi_{1}

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}-\sigma_{l}^{2}\right|\geq\left(1-\pi_{1}\right)v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[\left.-\left(1-\pi\right)^{2}v_{n}^{2}\right/\left(2n\omega_{l}^{4}\right)\right]

(S1.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{2}v_{n}^{2}/n\right)=\frac{C_{1}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

with
C_{1}=2,

C_{2}=\left.\left(1-\pi\right)^{2}\right/\left(2\omega_{l}^{4}\right),

C_{3}=2\lambda-1
, and last line holds by the fact
that
\lambda>1/2
. For
v_{n}\propto n^{\lambda}
with
\lambda>\left(1+s/2\right)/(2+s/2),
Lemma
A.2
implies

\Pr\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}-\sigma_{l}^{2}\right|\geq\left(1-\pi_{1}\right)v_{n}/n\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right),

(S1.9)

for some
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0.

We turn to the second term on the right hand side of (
S1.7
). Note
that Lemma

(to be shown below) still holds if we remove

\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}
and
\sigma_{l}^{2}
. Then by Lemma

\Pr\left(\left|n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right|\geq\pi_{1}v_{n}/n\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

(S1.10)

for some positive constants
C_{1},C_{2}
and
C_{3}
for
v_{n}\propto n^{\lambda}
with

5
5

5

It holds no matter which part of

A.6
we apply.

Combining (

), (
S1.8
), (
S1.9
), and

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.
We show the first part first. Note that

\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right|\leq\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{-2}\left\|\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\|\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right\|^{2},

(S1.11)

and we bound the three terms on the right hand side of (
S1.11
) in turn.
For the first term, with
v_{n}=\frac{1}{4}n\sigma_{l}^{2},
Lemma

A.5
implies

\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{-2}>\frac{4}{3}\sigma_{l}^{-2}\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{3}n^{C_{4}}\right),

(S1.12)

for some positive constants
C_{2},

C_{3},
and
C_{4}
. For the second
term, equations (
A.12
) and (
A.13
) and Assumption

7
imply that

\Pr\left(\left\|\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\|\geq\frac{4}{3}B_{1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\exp\left(-C_{3}nm_{n}^{-3}\right)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{2}\exp\left(-C_{3}n^{C_{4}}\right),

(S1.13)

for some positive constants
C_{2},

C_{3},
and
C_{4}
, and
C_{4}\leq 1-3B_{m}.
For the third term,

\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\phi_{j}\left(x_{li}\right)u_{li}\right\}^{2}.

As we discuss in the proof of Lemma
A.5
,
U_{l}
also satisfies
Assumption
4
. Because
\phi_{j}\left(x\right)
is uniformly
bounded for all
j,

\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)U_{l}
also satisfies
Assumption
4
. So

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right\|^{2}\geq\left(\frac{4}{3}B_{1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\phi_{j}\left(x_{li}\right)u_{li}\right\}^{2}\geq\frac{3}{4}B_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}\left(x_{li}\right)u_{li}\right\}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{m_{n}}\frac{3}{4}B_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{j}\left(x_{li}\right)u_{li}\right|\geq\left(\frac{3}{4}B_{1}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}^{1/2}n^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq m_{n}\exp\left(-\left.\left(1-\pi\right)^{2}\frac{3}{4}B_{1}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}n\right/\left[2n\left(C_{8}m_{n}^{-1}\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\left(1-\pi\right)^{2}\frac{3}{8}B_{1}C_{8}^{-1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}+\log m_{n}\right)

(S1.14)

for any
\pi\in\left(0,1\right),
where the second inequality holds by
the first part of Lemma
A.2
and Assumption

4
with
\alpha=s
and the fact that

\max_{j}\left\{E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}U_{l}^{2}\right]\right\}\leq\max_{j}\left\{E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}E(U_{l}^{2}|X_{l})\right]\right\}\leq C\max_{j}\left\{E\left[\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}\leq C_{8}m_{n}^{-1}

for some constants
C
and
C_{8}
by Assumption
6
. Here the second
inequality in the last displayed line follows from the fact that that
U_{l}

is
\varepsilon
plus a uniformly bounded term, and the last inequality holds
by Lemma
A.3
.

By (
S1.11
), (
S1.12
), (
S1.13
), and (
S1.14
),
we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\boldsymbol{u}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right|\geq\frac{4}{3}\sigma_{l}^{-2}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{-2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right\|^{2}\left\|\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\|\geq\frac{4}{3}\sigma_{l}^{-2}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l}^{-2}>\frac{4}{3}\sigma_{l}^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\left\|\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\|\geq\frac{4}{3}B_{1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{l}\right\|^{2}\geq\left(\frac{4}{3}B_{1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}+\log m_{n}\right)+C_{2}\exp\left(-C_{3}n^{C_{4}}\right)

for positive constants
C_{1}=\left(1-\pi\right)^{2}\frac{3}{8}B_{1}C_{8}^{-1},

C_{2}
,
C_{3}
and
C_{4}.
This completes the proof of the
first part of the lemma.

The second part holds similarly. The only difference is that we apply the

A.2
to the fourth line of equation
(
S1.14
).

Proof of Lemma

.

(i)
With Assumption

’,

Stone 1985

(S1.17)

Let

\displaystyle=E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{b}\right)\left(P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{b}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{1}^{b}-P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)^{\prime}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{b}\right)^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{1}^{b}\right]\right)\right]

This implies that

for any

By the

(S1.25)

If we have

\left\|\Phi_{\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}}\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}\right\|\gtrsim m_{n}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}\right\|\gtrsim m_{n}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{j}\right\|.

) and

S1.22

\left\|E\left[\left|P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}\right)\right|\left|\breve{R}_{n}\right|\right]\right\|\lesssim m_{n}^{-d}\left\|E\left[\left|P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}\right)\right|\right]\right\|\propto m_{n}^{-d-1/2}.

Using a similar argument as in (i), if
\left\{E\left[f_{nj}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}
for some

\left\|\Phi_{\boldsymbol{X}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}}\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{1}^{p^{\ast}}\right\|\gtrsim m_{n}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\breve{\beta}}_{j}\right\|\gtrsim\kappa_{n}\log\left(m_{n}\right)n^{-1/2},

and

is a small order term due to the fact that

by Assumption

. Thus

as desired.

Proof of Lemma
A.8
.
As in the proof of the last lemma, let

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}=E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)^{\prime}\right].
We prove the
lemma by showing that

\Pr\left(\left|\left\|\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\right\|-\left\|\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}\right\|\right|>\left.\left\|\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}\right\|\right/2\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\iota_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{1}nm_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}^{-1}\right\},

for a
\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)\times 1
random vector
\boldsymbol{Z}
,
where we have absorbed
X_{l}
into
\boldsymbol{Z}.
Assumption

10
applies to
\boldsymbol{Z}
. Because of this, the proof is
essentially the same as that for Lemma
A.4
. The only difference is
that the dimension of the matrix here is
m_{n}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)\times m_{n}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)
.

Following the proof in Lemma
A.4
up to equation (
S1.4
), we
have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right|\geq m_{n}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\max\left\{\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right|,\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}-n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)\right|\right\}\geq m_{n}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)v_{n}/n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}-\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|_{\infty}\geq v_{n}/n\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)^{2}\exp\left\{-v_{n}^{2}/2\left(B_{4}nm_{n}^{-1}+v_{n}/3\right)\right\}.

Set
v_{n}=nm_{n}^{-2}\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)^{-1}B_{X1}/3,
the above
inequality becomes

\Pr\left(\left|\lambda_{\min}\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)-\lambda_{\min}\left(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right|\geq B_{X1}m_{n}^{-1}/3\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\iota_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{1}nm_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}^{-1}\right\}

for some positive constant
C_{1}.

If in addition Assumption
2
’ and
7
holds,
nm_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}^{-1}\geq nm_{n}^{-3}p_{n}^{\ast\ast-1}\propto n^{1-3B_{m}-B_{p^{\ast\ast}}}=n^{C_{4}}
and
C_{4}>0.
The conclusion is
immediate.

Proof of Lemma
A.9
.
First

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq 2\sigma^{-2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)

(S1.28)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-2}\geq\frac{4}{3}\sigma^{-2}\right)+\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right).

Taking
v_{n}=\frac{1}{4}n\sigma^{2}
in Lemma
A.11
yields

\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-2}\geq\frac{4}{3}\sigma^{-2}\right)=\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}\leq\frac{3}{4}\sigma^{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}\exp(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}})

(S1.29)

for some positive
C_{1},C_{2},
and
C_{3}.
Noting that
\left(\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}
is the
lower block diagonal of
\left(\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[c]{cc}\mathbb{Z}&\mathbb{X}_{l}\end{array}\right)\right)^{-1}
, by Lemma
A.8
and Assumption

10
, we have

\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)\leq 2m_{n}^{2}\iota_{n}^{2}\exp\left\{-C_{1}nm_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}^{-1}\right\}.

Since
\iota_{n}\leq p^{\ast\ast},

nm_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}\leq n^{1-3B_{m}+B_{p^{\ast\ast}}}=n^{C_{3}}
and
C_{3}>0
by Assumption
2
’. Then

\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq\frac{3}{2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}\exp(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}).

(S1.30)

Combining (
S1.28
), (
S1.29
), and (
S1.30
) yields

\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq 2\sigma^{-2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)\leq C_{1}\exp(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}).

For the second part, we first make the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\lambda_{\min}\left\{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{4}\sigma^{-2}B_{X2}^{-1}m_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{-2}\right)+\Pr\left(\lambda_{\min}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{-2}B_{X2}^{-1}m_{n}\right).

Then we apply Lemma
A.11
and Lemma
A.8
to the two
terms on the right hand side of the last displayed equation. The conclusion
follows as in the proof of the first part.

Proof of Lemma
A.10
.
We deal with
n^{-1/2}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)
first. Note

n^{-1/2}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)

Denote the
j
-th element of
\boldsymbol{U}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}
by

U_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}.
By equation
(
A.9
) and Lemma
A.3
,
E\left(U_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}\right)

\propto m_{n}^{-1},
and

U_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}
is uniformly bounded
due to the uniform boundedness of
\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right).

By equation (
A.9
),

U_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}=\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)+\varepsilon-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\equiv\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)Y\right]
. The rank
conditions in Assumption
10
and uniform boundedness of

\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)
imply that each
element in
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}
is uniformly bounded. Note

p^{\ast}
is fixed and the dimension of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}
is
\iota_{n}m_{n}
, and thus

U_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}=C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}+\varepsilon,

with
C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\ast}}f_{j}^{\ast}\left(X_{j}\right)-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)
and
\left|C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right|\leq C\iota_{n}m_{n}
for a positive
C
. The
j
-th element of
\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}
is

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z,}i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\varepsilon_{i}.

Since
u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}
is uniformly
bounded,
\left|u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z,}i}\right|\leq C\iota_{n}m_{n}
for a positive
C,

and
u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\varepsilon_{i}

satisfies the tail restriction in Assumption
4
. Apply the
inequalities in Lemmas
A.1
on the first term in the above, we
obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)\right|>v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z,}i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)\right|>\frac{v_{n}}{2}\right)+\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\varepsilon_{i}\right|>\frac{v_{n}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left\{-v_{n}^{2}/\left(C\left(nm_{n}^{-1}+\iota_{n}m_{n}v_{n}\right)\right)\right\}+\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\varepsilon_{i}\right|>\frac{v_{n}}{2}\right).

(S1.31)

Using the above identity, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)\right\}^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)\right|\geq C_{1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left\{\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}C_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z,}i}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z,}j}\right)\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\text{ \ \ \ \ }\left.+\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\varepsilon_{i}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[c]{c}2m_{n}\exp\left\{-nm_{n}^{-2}v_{n}/[C_{2}(nm_{n}^{-1}+\iota_{n}n^{1/2}v_{n}^{1/2})]\right\}+m_{n}\exp\left(-C_{3}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)\\
2m_{n}\exp\left\{-nm_{n}^{-2}v_{n}/[C_{2}(nm_{n}^{-1}+\iota_{n}n^{1/2}v_{n}^{1/2})]\right\}+C_{4}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right)\end{array}\right.\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{if }v_{n}\lesssim n^{s/\left(s+2\right)}m_{n}^{2}\\
\text{if }v_{n}\gtrsim n^{s/\left(s+2\right)}m_{n}^{2}\end{array},

for any positive constant
C_{1}
and some positive constants
C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}
and
C_{5}
, where the last inequality follows from the same
arguments as used to obtain (
S1.14
) along with applying equation
(
S1.31
).

We turn to
n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}.
Note this is a
m_{n}\times 1

vector whose
j
-th element is
n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}

with the
n\times 1
vector
\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}
being an collecting of the
n
observations for
U_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}.
Then

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left(n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left(n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)^{2}\geq C_{1}nm_{n}^{-2}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\right\}^{2}\geq C_{1}nm_{n}^{-2}v_{n}\right).

(S1.36)

We deal with the three random terms in the last line in turn. First, by
Assumptions
10
and
2
’ and Lemma
A.8
, we
have

\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\right\}^{2}\geq\left(\frac{4}{3}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)^{2}\right)\leq C_{6}\exp\left(-C_{7}n^{C_{8}}\right)

(S1.37)

for some positive constants
C_{6},C_{7}
and
C_{8}.
For the second term,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{\iota_{n}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{m_{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\phi_{k_{2}}\left(z_{k_{1},i}\right)u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\right)^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{\iota_{n}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\phi_{k_{2}}\left(z_{k_{1},i}\right)u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\right)^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3}\iota_{n}^{-1}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{\iota_{n}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{m_{n}}\Pr\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{k_{2}}\left(z_{k_{1},i}\right)u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\right|\geq C_{9}n^{3/4}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1/2}v_{n}^{1/4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[c]{c}m_{n}\iota_{n}\exp\left(-C_{10}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1}\left(m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)^{1/2}\right)\\
C_{11}\exp\left(-C_{12}n^{C_{13}}\right)\end{array}\right.\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{if }v_{n}\lesssim n^{\left(s-2\right)/\left[4\left(s+2\right)\right]}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\\
\text{if }v_{n}\gtrsim n^{\left(s-2\right)/\left[4\left(s+2\right)\right]}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\end{array},

for some positive constants
C_{9},

C_{10},C_{11}
and
C_{12}
, where in
the last line we apply Lemma
A.2
and we use the fact that
var
\left(\phi_{k_{2}}\left(z_{k_{1},i}\right)u_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z},i}\right)\propto m_{n}^{-1}.
For the third
term, similarly,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{\iota_{n}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{m_{n}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}n^{-1/2}\phi_{k_{2}}\left(z_{k_{1},i}\right)u_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z},i}\right)^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[c]{c}m_{n}\iota_{n}\exp\left(-C_{10}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1}\left(m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)^{1/2}\right)\\
C_{11}\exp\left(-C_{12}n^{C_{13}}\right)\end{array}\right.\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{if }v_{n}\lesssim n^{\left(s-2\right)/\left[4\left(s+2\right)\right]}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\\
\text{if }v_{n}\gtrsim n^{\left(s-2\right)/\left[4\left(s+2\right)\right]}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\end{array},

where without loss of generality we use the same constants in equation
(
S1
).

Equation (
S1.36
) implies

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left\{\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\right\}^{2}\geq\left(\frac{4}{3}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)^{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\left.+\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{\phi_{j}\left(X_{l}\right),\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}^{1/2}\frac{3}{4}B_{X1}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-2}v_{n}^{1/2}\right)\right\}.

Substituting the results of equations (
S1.37
), (
S1
), and
(
S1
) into the above yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)

(S1.46)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[c]{l}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left[C_{6}\exp\left(-C_{7}n^{C_{8}}\right)+2m_{n}\iota_{n}\exp\left(-C_{10}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1}\left(m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)^{1/2}\right)\right]\\
\sum_{j=1}^{m_{n}}\left[C_{6}\exp\left(-C_{7}n^{C_{8}}\right)+2C_{11}\exp\left(-C_{12}n^{C_{13}}\right)\right]\end{array}\right.\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{if }v_{n}\lesssim n^{\frac{s-2}{4\left(s+2\right)}}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\\
\text{if }v_{n}\gtrsim n^{\frac{s-2}{4\left(s+2\right)}}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\end{array}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\begin{array}[c]{l}C_{13}\exp\left(-C_{14}n^{C_{15}}\right)+m_{n}^{2}\iota_{n}\exp\left(-C_{16}n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1}\left(m_{n}^{-1}v_{n}\right)^{1/2}\right)\\
C_{17}\exp\left(-C_{18}n^{C_{19}}\right)\end{array}\right.\begin{array}[c]{c}\text{if }v_{n}\lesssim n^{\frac{s-2}{4\left(s+2\right)}}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\\
\text{if }v_{n}\gtrsim n^{\frac{s-2}{4\left(s+2\right)}}m_{n}^{3/2}\iota_{n}^{2}\end{array},

for some positive constants
C_{13},\ldots,C_{19}.

Note that when
\upsilon_{n}=\varsigma_{n},

n^{1/2}m_{n}^{-3/2}\iota_{n}^{-1}\left(m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)^{1/2}\gg n^{1/2-1/6B_{m}-B_{p^{\ast\ast}}}=n^{C_{22}}
for a positive
C_{22}.

C_{22}
exists due to
Assumption
2
’. Then,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)

(S1.55)

\displaystyle\leq C_{20}\exp\left(-C_{21}n^{C_{22}}\right)

for some positive constants
C_{20},C_{21},
and
C_{22},
due to equation
(
S1.46
) and the above observation. Therefore, by equations
(
S1
) and (
S1.55
),

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\tilde{u}}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)-n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\left(\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)+\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}_{X_{l},\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{Y,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-C_{2}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}+\log m_{n}\right)+C_{20}\exp\left(-C_{21}n^{C_{22}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-M}+C_{20}\exp\left(-C_{21}n^{C_{22}}\right),

for an arbitrarily large constant
M,
where in the fourth line we use the
fact that the two terms in equation are of the same order due to
\iota_{n}n_{n}^{1/2}\varsigma_{n}^{1/2}\ll nm_{n}^{-1}
by Assumption
2
’.
This completes the proof of the lemma.

Proof of Lemma
A.11
.
To save notations, we absorb
X_{l}
into

\boldsymbol{Z}
which now becomes a
\left(\iota_{n}+1\right)\times 1

vector. Assumption
10
holds for
\boldsymbol{Z.}
Recall

\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}=E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)^{\prime}\right]
and
M_{\mathbb{Z}}=I_{n}-\mathbb{Z}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}.
By definition,

U=Y-P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)^{\prime}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)Y\right].

Let
u_{i}=Y_{i}-P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{z}_{i}\right)^{\prime}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{Z}}^{-1}E\left[P^{m_{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{Z}\right)Y\right]
and
\boldsymbol{u}=\left(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\right).
By the
definition of
\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2},
6
6

6

We defined

\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}
based on
\hat{u}_{i}
from partitioned
regressions. This
\hat{u}_{i}
is the same as the residual from the joint
regression.

\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}=n^{-1}\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}.

Then we are able to reach the same conclusion as in the proof of Lemma

A.5
, with the help of Lemma
A.10
.

Proof of Lemma
A.12
.
Note that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\boldsymbol{\tilde{u}}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{\prime}\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\tilde{u}}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\geq\varsigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\tilde{u}}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq\varsigma_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left\{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathbb{X}_{l}^{\prime}M_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{X}_{l}\right)^{-1}\right\}\geq 2\sigma^{-2}B_{X1}^{-1}m_{n}\right)

\displaystyle+\Pr\left(\left\|n^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\tilde{u}}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}}\right\|^{2}\geq\left(2\sigma^{-2}\right)^{-1}B_{X1}m_{n}^{-1}\varsigma_{n}\right).

Then we can reach the conclusion by applying Lemma
A.9
and the
last part of Lemma
A.10
.

Proof of Lemma
A.13
.
We prove the lemma by mathematical
induction. First, we show that the conclusion holds when
k=1,
viz., for the
first stage. We bound the probability of
\mathcal{N}_{1}
(one or more noise
variables are selected at stage 1) as

\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)=\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{L}_{l,1}\right)=\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right)\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right).

(S1.56)

By the definition in Table 2,
\theta_{l}\lesssim\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(m_{n}/n\right)^{1/2}
for noise variables, viz., for
p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1\leq l\leq p_{n}
. Then we are able to apply the first part of
Proposition
2.1
to bound
\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right)

for each
l\in[p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1,p_{n}]
to obtain

\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,1}\right)\leq n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

for any arbitrarily large constant
M
and some positive constants
C_{1},

C_{2}
and
C_{3}.
Then by Assumption
2
and (
S1.56
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left(n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq p_{n}n^{-M}+p_{n}C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-M_{1}}+C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right),

(S1.57)

where the last equality holds by some
0<M_{1}\leq M-B_{p}
and some constants

C_{5},C_{6}
and
C_{7}.
Since
M
is arbitrarily large and
B_{p}
is a
fixed number,
M_{1}
can also be arbitrarily large. Notice that
\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right),
we get

\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{1}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

We now show the conclusion for
k=2.
Notice that
\mathcal{N}_{2}=\mathcal{N}_{1}\cup\left\{\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}\right\}.
That is, some noise variables selected up to
and including stage 2 are either selected at stage 1 or at stage 2. To apply
Proposition
3.1
, we need
\mathcal{D}_{1}
: the pre-selected
variables are either signals or pseudo-signals at stage 2. In view of this and
the fact that
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{N}_{1}^{c}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\cup(\left\{\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}\right\}\cap\mathcal{D}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)+\Pr\left(\left\{\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}\right\}\cap\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)+\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}\cap\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)+\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}|\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{1}\right)+\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}|\mathcal{D}_{1}\right).

(S1.58)

where the first equality follows from the fact
\Pr\left(A\cup B\right)=\Pr\left(A\cup\left(B\cap A^{c}\right)\right)
for any two events
A

and
B
. Since the second term in (
S1.58
) is conditional on

\mathcal{D}_{1}
, the pre-selected variables in
\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(1\right)}
are either signals or pseudo signals. By Assumption

11
, the net effect of the noise variable
X_{l}
on
Y

with
\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(1\right)}
as pre-selected variables satisfies

\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(1\right)}}\lesssim\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}
. Then we are able to
apply the first part of Proposition
3.1
to obtain

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,2}|\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,2}|\mathcal{D}_{1}\right)\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left(n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-M_{1}}+C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right),

(S1.59)

where without loss of generality we use the same constants as in equation
(
S1.57
). Combining (
S1.57
)–(
S1.59
) yields

\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{2}\right)\leq 2n^{-M_{1}}+2C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right)

and

\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{2}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{2}\right)\geq 1-2n^{-M_{1}}-2C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

Now suppose we have shown the results for stage
k-1
with
k\geq 3,
and
obtained

\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k-1}\right)\leq\left(k-1\right)n^{-M_{1}}+\left(k-1\right)C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

(S1.60)

Note that
\mathcal{N}_{k}=\mathcal{N}_{k-1}\cup\left\{\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k}\right\},
viz., some noise
variables selected up to and including stage
k
are either selected at stage

k
or before stage
k
. Noting that
\mathcal{D}_{k-1}=\mathcal{N}_{k-1}^{c}

and following the derivation of equation (
S1.58
), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k-1}\cup\left(\left\{\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k}\right\}\cap\mathcal{D}_{k-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k-1}\right)+\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k}|\mathcal{D}_{k-1}\right).

(S1.61)

Conditioning on
\mathcal{D}_{k-1}
implies that the pre-selected variables

\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k-1\right)}
are either signals or pseudo signals
for stage
k
. By Assumption
11
again, the net effect of
the noise variable
X_{l}
on
Y
with
\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k-1\right)}

as pre-selected variables satisfies
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{l,\boldsymbol{Z}_{\left(k-1\right)}}\lesssim\log\left(m_{n}\right)^{1/2}m_{n}^{1/2}n^{-1/2}
. Applying the first part of Proposition
3.1
to
the second term in (
S1.61
) yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\cup_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\mathcal{B}_{l,k}|\mathcal{D}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\Pr\left(\mathcal{B}_{l,k}|\mathcal{D}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=p^{\ast}+p^{\ast\ast}+1}^{p_{n}}\left(n^{-M}+C_{1}\exp\left(-C_{2}n^{C_{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-M_{1}}+C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

(S1.62)

Combining (
S1.60
)–(
S1.62
), we obtain

\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k}\right)\leq kn^{-M_{1}}+kC_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right),

and

\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{k}\right)=1-\Pr\left(\mathcal{N}_{k}\right)\geq 1-kn^{-M_{1}}-kC_{7}\exp\left(-C_{5}n^{C_{6}}\right).

Apparently, when
k
is fixed or divergent to infinity at a rate no faster
than
n^{a}
for some
a>0,
we can write

\Pr\left(\mathcal{D}_{k}\right)\geq 1-n^{-M_{2}}-C_{7}\exp\left(-C_{8}n^{C_{9}}\right)\text{ for any }k\leq n^{a},

where
M_{2}
is a large positive constant,
M_{2}\leq M_{1}-a.

Appendix S2
Additional Simulation and Application Results

In this section we present some additional results for the simulation and application.

S2.1
Additional Simulation Results

In this subsection we report the simulation results for DGPs 2 - 5 and 7 - 10
in Tables
S1
–
S8
. The description of these DGPs and
the summary of the simulation results are given in Sections 4.1 and 4.3 of the
paper, respectively.

In order to investigate the finite sample performance of POST-OCMT procedure
compared to that of OCMT procedure under linear model, we consider the
following DGP.

DGP 11.

Four signals, many pseudo-signals, and one hidden
signal
. The covariates are generated as DGP 6
:

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=W_{j},\text{ }j=1,2,\text{ }

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{j}+U_{1}}{2},\text{ }j=3,4,

\displaystyle X_{5}

\displaystyle=U_{1},

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{4X_{1}+\left(j-5\right)W_{j-1}}{j-1},\text{
}j=6,10,14,18,\ldots,

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{4X_{2}+\left(j-5\right)W_{j-1}}{j-1},\text{
}j=7,11,15,19,\ldots,

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{4X_{3}+\left(j-5\right)W_{j-1}}{j-1},\text{
}j=8,12,16,20,\ldots,\text{ and}

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{4X_{4}+\left(j-5\right)W_{j-1}}{j-1},\text{
}j=9,13,17,21,\ldots

where
W_{j},

j=1,\ldots,p_{n}-1,
and
U_{1}
are independent draws from

U(0,1)
. The dependent variable
Y
is generated as

Y=3(X_{1}-0.5)+3(X_{2}-0.5)+6(X_{3}-0.25)+6\left(X_{4}-0.25\right)-3X_{5}+\varepsilon

where
\varepsilon
are independent draws from
N(0,1)
. Then,
p^{\ast}=5

with one hidden signal
(X_{5})
. In this DGP, we only report the results of
our procedure (OCMT and Post-OCMT). The simulation results for DGP 11 are
reported in Table
S9
. Clearly, from this table, we can see that
POST-OCMT works very well in picking the true signals including the hidden one
and has good out-sample forecasting performance, due to the fact that it
eliminates the impact of pseudo-signals.

S2.2
Additional Application Results

In this subsection, we first provide the description of the variables used in
the application and some summary statistics. Then we report the frequencies of
variables selected.

Table
S10
gives the description of the dependent variable and
covariates used in our application. Table
S11
reports the
summary statistics for the variables. Table
S12
reports
the frequencies of covariates selected by the methods under investigation. For
those variables that are not listed in Table
S12
, they are
not selected by any of these methods. Table
S12
suggests
that among the 78 covariates in the application, three of them, namely, G102,
G133, and G137, are all frequently selected by the multiple-stage OCMT,
post-OCMT, group Lasso and adaptive group Lasso.
